Base directory for this skill: /Users/user_c042661f/.claude/skills/artagents # ArtAgents A file-based toolkit for agents to make art and creative work alongside a human. Three kinds of beings live here: - **Executors** — run one concrete unit of work - **Orchestrators** — coordinate executors (and other orchestrators) into workflows - **Elements** — reusable render building blocks (effects, animations, transitions) `python3 -m artagents` is the executable package gateway. Every summons passes through this one gate. ## First commands Run from the repository root: ```bash git status --short python3 -m artagents --help python3 -m artagents doctor python3 -m artagents orchestrators list python3 -m artagents executors list python3 -m artagents elements list python3 -m artagents setup ``` `setup` is dry-run by default; pass `--apply` to mutate. ## Using tools Find an id: ```bash python3 -m artagents [executors|orchestrators|elements] list ``` Inspect to see inputs, outputs, and intent: ```bash python3 -m artagents [executors|orchestrators|elements] inspect <id> --json ``` Run it: ```bash python3 -m artagents [executors|orchestrators] run <id> -- <args> ``` Each tool has its own `STAGE.md` next to its `run.py`. That is the source of truth — read it before invoking. The JSON inspect output points at the folder root and `stage_file`; load only the one relevant `STAGE.md`, not all of them. ## Make something new Read `docs/creating-tools.md`, then follow this build order — every step before falling back to the next: 1. **Compose existing executors first.** `python3 -m artagents executors list` and `inspect` the candidates. If existing executors can be wired together to do the job, build only an orchestrator that calls them. 2. **Create the missing executors next.** Each new executor must do *one* concrete, focused unit of work — independently runnable, inspectable, and testable. Don't pack workflow logic into an executor; that belongs in an orchestrator. 3. **Then write the orchestrator.** It composes the executors (existing + newly created) into the workflow. Orchestrators may call other orchestrators; executors must not. Skipping step 1 to write a god-orchestrator that bakes in network calls and business logic is the anti-pattern. Suspect it whenever a `run.py` grows past a couple of hundred lines without delegating to executors. Templates: - `docs/templates/executor/` — one concrete unit of work - `docs/templates/orchestrator/` — a workflow that combines executors - `docs/templates/element/` — a reusable render building block Public folders have exactly one format: `artagents/orchestrators/<slug>/{orchestrator.yaml,STAGE.md,run.py}` and `artagents/executors/<slug>/{executor.yaml,STAGE.md,run.py}`, with optional local `src/` modules. Top-level `artagents/*.py` files are shared libraries or system commands, not alternate runnable […]

> AGENT

Looked at `runs/` — each run is a flat sibling folder with no notion of "this v2 belongs with v1." Here's the shape I'd recommend: **Abstraction: `workstream` = a folder of runs + a manifest.** Layout: `runs/<workstream-slug>/<NNN-run-slug>/...` with a `runs/<slug>/workstream.json` carrying `{id, intent, type, status: open|closed, started, closed, run_ids[]}`. A single pointer file `runs/.active` names the currently open one. New runs auto-nest into `.active` — zero friction for the common case (iterating). **Switch behaviour: gate on `status`, not on type match.** Type-matching sounds nice but breaks the natural cross-tool flow of one idea (generate → cut → render is one workstream across three types). Instead: if `.active` is open and you start a new run, just append. If you run `workstream start <new>`, the CLI prints "workstream `X` is still open with N runs — close, pause, or run in parallel?" Auto-close after some idle threshold (say 24h) so stale streams don't trap you. Type lives on the workstream as metadata, not as a grouping key. **Timeline integration:** when `cut`/`render` writes a timeline, stamp `provenance: {workstream_id, run_ids[], snapshot_path}` into `hype.timeline.json`, and copy a frozen `workstream.json` next to the rendered video. That way the saved artifact carries the full iteration trail — every brief tweak, regen, parameter sweep — without depending on `runs/` still existing. **Main tradeoff:** the auto-nest default is the whole point (frictionless), but it means a forgotten-open workstream silently absorbs unrelated work. The idle-timeout + a one-line banner on each run (`→ workstream: artagents_logo (run 5, opened 2h ago)`) is the cheapest mitigation — visibility without prompts. Want me to sketch the `workstream.json` schema and the CLI surface, or prototype the auto-nesting in the runner?

Read the actual chokepoints. Here's the design, fully specified. --- # Workstreams: design ## Goals / non-goals **Goals.** 1. Every run is automatically attributed to a workstream with zero in-flow user input. 2. Re-attribution (split/merge/move) is a metadata edit, never a file move. 3. Saved timelines carry full ancestry independent of `runs/` existing. 4. Concurrent runs are correct under filesystem locks. 5. Backwards compatible: every existing `runs/*/` keeps working with no migration required. **Non-goals (v1).** - Branching UI / tree visualisation. Edges are stored; render is later. - Cross-machine sync. Workstream state is per-repo, alongside `.artagents/`. - Heuristic split suggestions (input-overlap clustering). Ship the data; teach later. - Renaming/repathing runs across workstreams via folder ops. Use the CLI. --- ## Data model Two persisted shapes, both versioned. ### `runs/<slug>/run.json` (one per run, written at run start) ```json { "schema_version": 1, "run_id": "01HXKQ8ZN8M2P1S7K3X4Y5Z6A7", "workstream_id": "01HXKQ4VJ7B0Q9K2P8X1Y4Z3M5", "parent_run_id": "01HXKQ7M2J3K5P8X1Y4Z6A9B0C", "executor_id": "builtin.cut", "orchestrator_id": null, "kind": "executor", "out_path": "runs/artagents_logo_v3", "started_at": "2026-05-03T14:22:11Z", "ended_at": "2026-05-03T14:25:03Z", "returncode": 0, "dry_run": false, "inputs_digest": "sha256:...", "input_artifacts": [ {"path": "runs/artagents_logo_v2/hype.timeline.json", "sha256": "...", "produced_by_run_id": "01HXK..."}, {"path": "briefs/logo.txt", "sha256": "...", "produced_by_run_id": null} ], "output_artifacts": [ {"path": "hype.timeline.json", "sha256": "...", "kind": "timeline"}, {"path": "hype.assets.json", "sha256": "...", "kind": "assets"} ], "cli_args_redacted": ["builtin.cut", "--out", "runs/artagents_logo_v3", "--from-step", "render"], "agent_version": "git:d1ca868" } ``` ULIDs (lex-sortable, 26 chars, monotonic within a process). `parent_run_id` is the last run in the same workstream at the moment this one started — that's the lineage edge for tree rendering later. ### `.artagents/workstreams.json` (single index, append-mostly) ```json { "schema_version": 1, "active_workstream_id": "01HXKQ4VJ7B0Q9K2P8X1Y4Z3M5", "workstreams": { "01HXKQ4VJ7B0Q9K2P8X1Y4Z3M5": { "id": "01HXKQ4VJ7B0Q9K2P8X1Y4Z3M5", "label": "artagents logo", "label_source": "auto:first_brief", "status": "open", "created_at": "2026-05-01T10:11:00Z", "last_run_at": "2026-05-03T14:25:03Z", "archived_at": null, "merged_into": null, "run_ids": ["01HXKQ...", "01HXKR...", "01HXKS..."] } } } ``` Status lifecycle: `open` → `archived` → (re)`open`. Plus terminal `merged_into=<id>` (no longer accepts runs; reads redirect). Why a single file: the index is small (KB range for years of use), atomic-replaceable, and trivially lockable. A directory of per-workstream files would multiply the locking surface and complicate the active-pointer. ### Identity rules - **`run_id`**: ULID. The only stable handle. - **`workstream_id`**: ULID. Stable across renames. - **`label`**: human-readable, mutable, non-unique. Never used as a key. - **`out_path`**: a hint, not a key. Renames don't break the world. - **References everywhere are by id**, never by path or label. --- ## Lifecycle / auto-grouping algorithm Single decision function, called once at run start under the lock: ``` def attribute(now, requested) -> (workstream_id, parent_run_id, was_created): state = […]

Both critiques landed hard. Here's what I'm taking, what I'm pushing back on, and the revised design. --- ## What I accept (and why) ### 1. Rename `workstream` → `thread` The noun is what the agent reads in every tool output. "Thread" matches the creator's head-state ("I want to pull on this thread") and is shorter to type. Git collision is real but mostly survivable — the tool's domain is creative work, not VCS, and context disambiguates. **Accepted.** ### 2. `parent_run_ids` (plural, causal, hash-derived) — not temporal The temporal "previous run in this thread" was always a lie during interleaved work. Compute parents from input-artifact hashes against producer `run_id`s. A run with no consumed artifacts has empty `parent_run_ids` — that's a *root*, which is the truth. **Accepted.** ### 3. Backfill by hash-graph connected components, not mtime Mtime clusters are dishonest. Hash every existing output, build the artifact DAG by re-scanning declared input args + content addressing, and present connected components as candidate threads for the user to label. **Accepted.** ### 4. `starred: bool` on runs and `canonical_run_id` on threads, day one Two fields. Prevents a schema migration when the user inevitably asks "which v is the keeper?" Creators iterate to *select*, not to converge. **Accepted.** ### 5. Structured `[thread]` prefix on every run command's stdout This is the single highest-leverage change. The agent reads its own tool output every turn — putting thread state in that output makes ignoring it impossible. **Accepted.** ### 6. Tiered Notice/Warn before run output Notice on first run of a session / >1h gap / cwd change. Warn on brief-novelty against thread history. Errors only on real problems (lock contention, corruption). **Accepted, with discipline:** if Warn fires more than ~5% of runs in practice, it's broken — agents learn to ignore noisy warnings. ### 7. Parent-lineage inference from input args overrides `@active` If `--brief` or `--asset` or `--video` points inside `runs/<some-prior-run>/`, that run's thread becomes the strong default. This is the cleanest, cheapest auto-attribute upgrade and removes a whole class of mis-grouping. **Accepted.** ### 8. `WORKSTREAMS.md` (one global doc) + footer in `executors inspect` + one sentence in `SKILL.md` Don't pollute every `STAGE.md`. The runtime prefix is the real documentation. **Accepted.** ### 9. `thread show @active` documented as the session-opener First move of any session that will produce runs. One sentence in `SKILL.md`. Output a parseable structured block. **Accepted.** ### 10. `thread doctor` — passive, […]

> AGENT

Four agents back. Strong convergence and the design is now concrete enough to commit to. Direct answers to your questions, then the integrated plan. --- ## Answering your questions **Q: Will it create a video showing iteration for both items and timelines?** Yes via `builtin.iteration_video`, but with one honest limit. Item iterations (images, generations) render beautifully — stills, grids that resolve to chosen, prompt-diff overlays. **Timeline iterations are second-class no matter what you do** because each is a 90-second artifact you have ~3 seconds to show. The orchestrator handles them via four visual modes (`scrub`, `pip`, `playthrough_4x`, chapter-card) and an HTML report sibling that's the *real* artifact for timeline-heavy threads. Be explicit in docs: timeline-heavy threads produce a *guided index* video, not a watch-through. **Q: Will it reliably track variants?** Yes, by making executors **declare** variant role at output time, defaulting everything to `role: ancillary`, and failing closed. Multi-output executors (`generate_image -n 4`, `logo_ideas --count 9`) opt into `role: variant` and auto-emit a `group` id at run time. Heterogeneous outputs (4 candidates + grid + manifest) cannot accidentally poison the variant group because anything not declared `variant` is excluded by default. **Q: Reliable + easy for agents to adhere to?** The agent's per-attempt floor is **one gesture**: `thread keep <run-id>:<n>`. Zero if the run produced one output. Five gestures total in the whole vocabulary. The `[variants]` prefix line persists on every subsequent run until the agent resolves it — pollution is the right tradeoff because an unresolved variant group is real debt. **Q: Default aligned + obvious when misaligned?** Aligned defaults: auto-group sibling outputs of every multi-output run; auto-attribute every run to a thread; lineage inference from input paths; never auto-pick. Misalignment surfaces in four loud places — the `[variants]` nag (per-run), `[thread-health]` smell (on `thread list`), `[hint]` for suspected manual fan-out (after 3 sequential same-executor runs), and the iteration-video quality floor refusing to render lying videos. **Q: What does the orchestrator+executors look like?** Concrete spec below. Three new small executors plus heavy reuse of `builtin.hype` / `builtin.render`. --- ## Integrated additions to the plan ### 1. Variant primitive — minimal, no new entity Extend `output_artifacts` in `run.json` instead of adding a noun: ```diff "output_artifacts": [ - {"path": "candidate_01.png", "sha256": "...", "kind": "image"}, + {"path": "candidate_01.png", "sha256": "...", "kind": "image", + "role": "variant", "group": "g_<hash>", "group_index": 0, + "variant_meta": {"prompt": "...", "seed": 12345, "name": "Ascending Triad"}}, + {"path": "contact_sheet.png", "sha256": […]

Agent's report is sharp. Here's the integration. ## Direct answers **Can it interpret different types of videos?** Yes, via a **modality renderer registry** that replaces the hardcoded `(shape × style → mode)` decision table. Each modality (image, video, audio, text, 3D, code) gets its own renderer declaring what it consumes and what previews it can produce. The orchestrator's only global decision becomes "which renderer per modality" — and that's resolved from artifact capabilities, not guessed. **Different ways to present them?** Three flags plus one inspect command cover it: `iteration_video inspect <thread>` to see what's detected, `--renderers image=grid,audio=waveform` to override, `--clip-mode <run-id>=pip` to fix one specific iteration, `--direction "..."` for natural-language creative direction. Themes win automatically when set on the thread. **All modalities?** Yes — image, video, audio, text/markdown, 3D, code, voice are first-class. A `generic_card` fallback handles anything unknown (filename + kind badge + mtime + prompt excerpt, 2.5s hold). Never silently skip — a missing iteration is a lie about the process. ## Integrated additions ### New primitive: `ModalityRenderer` Lives at `artagents/modalities/` (sibling to `elements/`, `executors/`, `orchestrators/`). Each renderer is a small declarative file: ``` kinds: ["png", "jpg", "webp"] clip_modes: ["still", "grid", "ken_burns"] default_clip_mode_for(shape, style) # renderer's own scoped decision table produces_audio: false cost_hint: cheap | medium | expensive ``` v1 ships seven: `image_grid`, `audio_waveform`, `video_pip`, `text_diff`, `model_turntable`, `code_scroll`, `generic_card`. Discoverable via `python3 -m artagents modalities list` / `inspect`, paralleling `executors`/`orchestrators`. ### Capability declarations on artifacts (extends the variant addition) ```diff "output_artifacts": [ {"path": "song_v3.wav", "sha256": "...", "kind": "audio", "role": "variant", "group": "g_<hash>", + "preview_modes": ["waveform", "excerpt_3s", "full_play"], + "duration": 124.0, "variant_meta": {...}} ] ``` Producers (executors that write artifacts) declare what previews are tractable. Renderer registry resolves `(kind, preview_modes)` to a concrete renderer at assembly time. This is the missing capability layer and removes the orchestrator's guesswork. ### Agent surface (the only flags that matter) ``` iteration_video inspect <thread> Prints: detected modalities, dominant modality, suggested style, chosen renderer per modality, default clip-mode per iteration, estimated runtime. THE primary discovery tool — read once, then drive. --renderers image=grid,audio=waveform,video=pip --clip-mode <run-id>=pip # single-iteration override --direction "contact sheet from a darkroom" # natural-language creative direction --mode chaptered|parallel|interleaved # for cross-modal threads (default: chaptered) --audio-bed iterations|theme|silence|<path> ``` **Precedence:** theme (sticky brand) > direction (one-off intent) > style preset > defaults. ### Cross-modal threads: pursuit tracks + chapters Extend the thread layer with optional **sub-pursuits** (e.g., music-video thread = `{song, cover, video}`). Base directory for this skill: /Users/user_c042661f/.claude/skills/megaplan # Megaplan Route every step through the `megaplan` CLI. Never call agents directly. Before the first CLI call, resolve a working launcher and reuse it for the whole run. Do not assume `megaplan` itself is on `PATH`; command presence alone is not enough. Prove the launcher works by successfully running a harmless CLI call with it first. In the instructions below, treat `<launcher>` as that verified command. Launcher resolution order: 1. Try `python -m megaplan config show`. 2. If that fails, try `./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan config show`. 3. If that fails, try `uv run python -m megaplan config show`. 4. If that fails, try a version-selected shim such as `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan config show`. 5. Only use bare `megaplan ...` if that exact form already succeeded during this check. ## Triage A single megaplan can cover as much as 2 weeks of work — don't reflexively split large efforts into multiple plans. Pick the right level based on the task: - **Skip megaplan**: single-file fixes, bug fixes with clear cause, simple refactors, config changes, adding tests for existing code. Just do it. - **Light**: multi-file changes with clear scope, well-understood features, straightforward additions. One critique pass, no gate, no review. - **Standard** (default for megaplan): cross-cutting changes touching many subsystems, unfamiliar codebase areas, ambiguous requirements, changes with high breakage risk, or anything where the plan itself needs debate. - **Heavy**: high-stakes changes where getting it wrong is expensive — security-critical code, data migrations, public API changes. Uses the same visible `prep` phase but with 8 critique checks instead of 4. Default to standard unless the task is clearly simple enough for light. Do not ask the user to choose robustness — pick it yourself based on the above. Only ask execution mode (auto-approve or review) when using megaplan. ## Modes Megaplan has two output modes, picked with `--mode` at `init`: - **`--mode code`** (default): the run produces a code diff. Execute workers emit per-task file changes. Use for features, refactors, bug fixes, migrations — anything whose deliverable is source code. - **`--mode metaplan`** (alias: `--mode doc`): the run produces a single document artifact at `--output <relative/path>` (e.g. `docs/design.md`). The prep, execute, and review phases use authoring-specific prompts; the execute schema uses `sections_written` instead of file changes; auditing reasons about section delivery. Use for design docs, architecture specs, research notes, RFCs, proposals, post-mortems, migration plans — […]

Don't ship enum values whose only consumer is the writer. ### D. `preview_modes` array on every output artifact SD-026 has each artifact declare `preview_modes: ["still", "grid", "waveform", ...]` so the renderer registry can resolve `(kind, preview_modes) → renderer`. With three renderers and one-mode-each, **`kind` alone is the dispatch key.** A `.png` goes to image, `.wav` goes to audio, everything else to generic. **Trim:** drop `preview_modes` from output_artifacts in v1. Add the field when there's a renderer demanding it (a 3D model that wants both `turntable` and `wireframe`, say). Reframing under your criterion: **complexity in agent interactions or data shapes for little return.** Time isn't the metric; agent cognitive load and data-surface stability are. Status note first: subagent finished with plan `finalized`, but execute hasn't run yet — `docs/sprint-thread-layer.md` doesn't exist. So we have a clean window to apply trims before the doc is written. ## Cuts I'd make under your criterion **1. Warn tier with brief-novelty heuristic.** Adds a noisy signal class with a tunable threshold. False positives train agents to ignore Warn. **Notice tier alone is enough in v1.** One fewer line format for agents to parse and discount. **2. `[thread-health]` smell line on `thread list`.** Adds a parseable output format. Detection-only nudge. **Cut entirely** — agents don't need health detection in v1; the data's still there for a follow-up. **3. `[hint]` line for sequential fan-out.** Same — another output format the agent has to recognize. Soft nudge value, real parsing burden. **Cut.** **4. Four `role` values (`variant`/`ancillary`/`manifest`/`index`) → two (`variant` + `other`).** Agents stamping outputs see only one meaningful distinction (variant vs not). The other three values are vocabulary an agent has to learn for zero behavior change. **Trim to two.** **5. `preview_modes: [...]` array on every `output_artifact`.** Declaration burden on every executor for zero v1 dispatch value — three renderers all key off `kind` alone. **Drop the field in v1.** Add when there's a renderer that needs it (3D model offering both turntable and wireframe). **6. `chosen_from_groups` as a separate top-level edge from `parent_run_ids`.** Collapses cleanly into typed `parent_run_ids` entries: `[{run_id, kind: "causal"}, {run_id, kind: "chosen", group: "g_..."}]`. **One edge collection in run.json instead of two.** Agents reading provenance see a single field, not a join. **7. Four iteration-video executors → two.** `iteration.prepare` (collect + summarize + score) + `iteration.assemble`. Each new executor is a STAGE.md, an executor.yaml, a CLI entry, an agent has to read about. **Halves the surface area an agent inspects.** Keep the split where there's real v1 reuse value (assemble vs prepare); fuse the rest. **8. `external_service_calls` six fields → three.** Keep `model`, `model_version`, `request_id` (the irreversible provenance bits). Drop `seed`, `cost_usd`, `latency_ms` — they're observability without a v1 consumer. **Cleaner data shape.** **9. Cost block low/high range + one-line recommendation → single number.** `Estimated cost: ~$0.42 (47 calls × $0.009)`. **Easier to parse, no triplet to interpret.** **10. `host_id` field.** No v1 or v2 consumer (cross-machine sync is deferred). # /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it. ## Parsing (in priority order) 1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt. 2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). Run: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan execute --plan plan-a-focused-2-week-10-20260503-1748 --confirm-destructive (in background). Then ScheduleWakeup at 900s. - active_step present with health=="healthy" and age_seconds < 1500: phase is in flight, just wait. ScheduleWakeup at max(600, 1500 - age_seconds) so the next check lands shortly after the typical max duration. - active_step present with health!="healthy" or age_seconds > 1800: stale phase — report the issue, stop the loop, ask the user how to proceed. - state in {"planned","critiqued","gated"} with no active_step: workflow is paused between phases. Drive forward with the appropriate command (critique → gate → revise → finalize per state). Then ScheduleWakeup at 600s. - Anything unexpected: report to user, stop the loop. 3. If state OR next_step OR iteration changed since last wake: relay one short sentence to the user before scheduling the next check. 4. NEVER call ScheduleWakeup with delay <= 300 (cache-window-burn rule). Default to 1200s when uncertain. 5. When stopping the loop, write a clear final summary line ("LOOP STOPPED: <reason>") so the user knows checking has ceased. PLAN ID FOR ALL CALLS: plan-a-focused-2-week-10-20260503-1748</command-args>

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it. ## Parsing (in priority order) 1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt. 2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). If they pick **This session only**: - […]

IMPORTANT: if last_step.result was "error" twice in a row, STOP loop and report worker bug. Otherwise ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - state == "prepped" with next_step == "plan": run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan plan --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020` in background, ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - active_step present with health=="healthy" and age_seconds < 1500: phase in flight. # /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it. ## Parsing (in priority order) 1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt. 2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). If they pick **This session only**: - […]

> AGENT

Yes — this is high-quality. Specifics: **Convergence shape is excellent**: 19.75 → 4.0 → 0 weighted score, 17 flags resolved, 0 open, 0 recurring critiques across 3 iterations. The plan grew 138→258 lines as gaps closed without scope creep. **Real risks were caught and patched, not papered over:** - **Render contract bug** (Step 6 overview, Step 20.9-10, Step 21.2-3): critique discovered `builtin.render` is hardwired to `hype.timeline.json`/`hype.assets.json`/`hype.mp4` — not a generic renderer for `iteration.timeline.json`. Plan now correctly makes `iteration.assemble` an *adapter* that emits both `iteration.*` and `hype.*` so render works unchanged, then renames `hype.mp4` → `iteration.mp4`. - **Provenance duplication** (Step 5.4, Step 15.3): caught that `artagents/audit/*` already maintains a parent-ID ledger. Plan now bridges instead of inventing a parallel graph. - **Forbidden file-mod risk** (Step 15.4, Step 21): hype provenance gets injected via wrapper/orchestrator, not by editing executor `run.py` outside the SD-031 opt-in (`generate_image`, `logo_ideas`). - **`thread gc` contradiction** (Step 3.4): plan v1 still referenced `thread gc --force-unlock` for stale-lock recovery despite SD-036 cutting it; now removed with explicit test (Step 4.2). - **Missing CI fixture** (Step 24): caught that `runs/artagents_logo_v3` is gitignored — added a tracked compact fixture so review/CI can verify, with the real run kept as local dogfood. - **Edge cases**: `out=""`/`out=None` handling, nested subprocess `ARTAGENTS_THREAD_ID` propagation beyond `_run_external_executor`, top-level routing in `pipeline.py`, locked `groups.json` writes, unconditional `xxhash`. **Honest limits worth flagging:** - Step 17.4 says "do not introduce `preview_modes`" — that's defending DEF trims, good. - Step 24.5 keeps the 60-second video playback as info-level, not CI must-gate. That's correct (you can't visually validate in CI) but it means the "does it actually look right" check still falls on you during dogfood. - Plan is verbose (258 lines, 25 steps) — execute phase will be large. Expect 30-90+ min and meaningful cost. Net: this is the kind of plan that catches its own bugs before code lands. Going into execute now is the right move.

> DEVELOPER

but qualitatively

> AGENT

Honest read: it's a B+ plan, not an A. **What it gets right architecturally:** - The **render-adapter insight** (Step 20.9-10) is the smart move. Recognizing `builtin.render` is hardwired to `hype.*` filenames and making `iteration.assemble` emit both the canonical `iteration.*` files *and* render-compatible `hype.*` adapter files — instead of forking render — is the kind of choice that avoids a months-long maintenance bill. - **Bridging audit provenance** instead of building a parallel `parent_run_ids` graph is correct. Two provenance sources would diverge within a week. - **Chokepoint discipline** is being treated seriously: explicit audit step (25.2) confirms no executor `run.py` outside SD-031 was touched. - DEF trims aren't just listed, they're actively *defended* — Step 17.4 explicitly says "do not introduce `preview_modes`," Step 25.3 audits for trimmed fields. **Where it's still hand-wavy on the load-bearing parts:** - **Provenance bridge (Step 5.4, 15.3)** — the single most important architectural decision in the doc gets ~3 bullets. Actual mapping from `ARTAGENTS_AUDIT_PARENT_IDS` → typed `parent_run_ids[]` edges isn't specified. Execute will improvise here. - **`groups.json` lock strategy (13.2)** — "either share the thread index lock or use a per-thread groups lock." That's a deferred decision dressed as a step. It matters because it determines whether eight concurrent variant writers serialize on one global lock. - **`hype.mp4` → `iteration.mp4` (21.4)** — "copy or rename… unless render can safely target `iteration.mp4` directly." Hedging means execute picks whichever is easier and you may end up with both files in `runs/`. - **HTML report quality (21.10)** — one of the trickier UX deliverables and it gets one bullet. Critique didn't push hard on report polish. - **Cost-per-call constant (18.8, 21.6)** — placeholder, not a number. Small thing but "we'll figure it out later" smell. **Execution risk:** - 25 steps, 14 marked "Scope: Large", in 10 sprint-days. That's tight. - "Read its own new STAGE.md before invoking" (18.1, 20.1) is slightly muddled — the executor doesn't *have* a STAGE.md yet because you're creating it. Suggests the bootstrap order wasn't fully chewed. - Tests are well-distributed but often "verify X" without scenarios. **Net:** the bones are good and the worst architectural traps are caught. The HTML report and the audit-provenance bridge are the two places where execute will have to do real engineering rather than follow instructions, and those are exactly the things you'll want to eyeball during dogfood. Worth shipping; not worth running another revise loop.

Run: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('State:', d.get('state')); print('Iteration:', d.get('iteration')); print('Next step:', d.get('next_step')); print('Cost USD:', round(d.get('total_cost_usd',0),2)); last=d.get('last_step') or {}; print('Last step:', last.get('step'),'-',last.get('result'),'@',last.get('timestamp')); active=d.get('active_step') or {}; (print('Active:', active.get('step'),'agent='+str(active.get('agent')),'age='+str(active.get('age_seconds'))+'s','health='+str(active.get('health'))) if active else None); (print(' Progress:',active.get('phase_progress_summary')) if active else None); print('Notes:', d.get('notes_count'))" 2. Decide next action by state+next_step: - state == "done" or state == "reviewed": LOOP COMPLETE. Run `git status --short`, summarize what shipped, report total cost, stop loop. - next_step == "review" (state="executed"): run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan review --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020` in background, ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "execute" (state="finalized"): run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan execute --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 --confirm-destructive` in background. Heavy phase 30-90+min. ScheduleWakeup 1800s. - next_step == "revise": run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan revise --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020` in background. WATCH FOR INFINITE LOOP: if iteration > 3, STOP and ask user. ScheduleWakeup 1200s. - next_step == "finalize": run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan finalize --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020` in background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - next_step == "gate": run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan gate --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020` in background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "critique": run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan critique --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020` in background. If last_step.result was "error" twice in a row, STOP and report worker bug. Otherwise ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "plan": run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan plan --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020` in background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - active_step present with health=="healthy" and age_seconds < 1500: phase in flight. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800 - age_seconds). - active_step present with health!="healthy" or age_seconds > 1800: stale phase — report, stop loop. - Anything unexpected: report, stop loop. 3. If state OR next_step OR iteration changed since last wake: relay one short sentence to user. 4. NEVER ScheduleWakeup with delay <= 300 (cache-window-burn rule). Default 1200s. 5. When stopping, write "LOOP STOPPED: <reason>".</command-args>

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it. ## Parsing (in priority order) 1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt. 2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). If they pick **This session only**: - […]

Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. PLAN ID: implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 LAUNCHER: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ... EACH WAKE: 1. Check status: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('State:', d.get('state')); print('Iteration:', d.get('iteration')); print('Next step:', d.get('next_step')); print('Cost USD:', round(d.get('total_cost_usd',0),2)); last=d.get('last_step') or {}; print('Last step:', last.get('step'),'-',last.get('result'),'@',last.get('timestamp')); active=d.get('active_step') or {}; (print('Active:', active.get('step'),'agent='+str(active.get('agent')),'age='+str(active.get('age_seconds'))+'s','health='+str(active.get('health'))) if active else None); (print(' Progress:',active.get('phase_progress_summary')) if active else None); print('Notes:', d.get('notes_count'))" 2. Decide by next_step (authoritative): - state in {"done","reviewed"}: LOOP COMPLETE. `git status --short`, summarize what shipped, report cost. STOP. - next_step == "review": run `megaplan review --plan ...` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - next_step == "execute": run `megaplan execute --plan ... --confirm-destructive --user-approved` background. Heavy 30-90+min. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "finalize": run `megaplan finalize --plan ...` foreground. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "revise": if iteration > 3, STOP and ask user. Otherwise run `megaplan revise --plan ...` background. ScheduleWakeup 1200s. - next_step == "gate": run `megaplan gate --plan ...` foreground. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "critique": run `megaplan critique --plan ...` background. If last 2 critique results were "error", STOP and report worker bug. Otherwise ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "plan": run `megaplan plan --plan ...` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "prep": run `megaplan prep --plan ...` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - active_step healthy and age < 1500: wait. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800-age). - active_step age > 1800 or unhealthy: stop loop, ask user. - Anything unexpected: stop loop, ask user. All commands need `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11` prefix. 3. Relay state changes briefly. 4. NEVER ScheduleWakeup <= 300. 5. On stop, write "LOOP STOPPED: <reason>".</command-args>

Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). Check status: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('State:', d.get('state')); print('Iteration:', d.get('iteration')); print('Next step:', d.get('next_step')); print('Cost USD:', round(d.get('total_cost_usd',0),2)); last=d.get('last_step') or {}; print('Last step:', last.get('step'),'-',last.get('result'),'@',last.get('timestamp')); active=d.get('active_step') or {}; (print('Active:', active.get('step'),'agent='+str(active.get('agent')),'age='+str(active.get('age_seconds'))+'s','health='+str(active.get('health'))) if active else None); (print(' Progress:',active.get('phase_progress_summary')) if active else None); print('Notes:', d.get('notes_count'))" For execute also: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan progress --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); tasks=d.get('tasks',[]); from collections import Counter; c=Counter(t.get('status','?') for t in tasks); print('Batches:',dict(c))" 2. Decide by next_step (authoritative): - state in {"done","reviewed"}: LOOP COMPLETE. `git status --short`, summarize, report cost. STOP. - next_step == "review": run `megaplan review` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "execute": run `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved` background. ScheduleWakeup 1800s. - next_step == "finalize": run `megaplan finalize` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "revise": if iteration > 4, STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan revise` background. ScheduleWakeup 1200s. - next_step == "gate": run `megaplan gate` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "critique": if last 2 critique results were "error", STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan critique` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "plan": run `megaplan plan` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "prep": run `megaplan prep` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - active_step healthy and active_step.step == "execute": allow up to 5400s. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 5400-age). - active_step healthy other phases: allow up to 1800s. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800-age). - active_step age past max OR unhealthy: STOP and ask user. - Anything unexpected: stop loop, ask user. All commands need PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 prefix and --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020. 3. Relay state changes briefly. For execute, mention batch progress if changed (note: status shows "execute running" but progress json has the actual batches done/pending count). 4. NEVER ScheduleWakeup <= 300. 5. On stop, write "LOOP STOPPED: <reason>".</command-args>

> DEVELOPER

What do the first 5 cover?

> AGENT

6/15 batches done (gained 1 in the last 2 min). The first 6 cover all of **Week 1 — the data layer**: - **T1 — Audit & smoke checks.** Inspected `artagents/pipeline.py`, runner chokepoints (`executors/runner.py:75`, `orchestrators/runner.py:120`), audit provenance flow, render contract (`hype.timeline.json`/`hype.assets.json`/`hype.mp4`). No code changed; baseline `pytest`/`doctor` passed. - **T2 — Foundation.** Added `xxhash>=3.4` to `requirements.txt`. Created `artagents/threads/{ids,schema,index}.py` with Crockford ULIDs, schema_version 1 validators, repo-relative path enforcement, and `.artagents/threads.json` with `fcntl.flock` + 30s timeout + atomic write + fsync + `.bak` rotation. 8 new tests pass. - **T3 — Failure-safe records & chokepoint wrap.** Added `threads/record.py`, `threads/wrapper.py`. Wrapped `run_executor()` and `run_orchestrator()` with try/except/finally; preserved no-op gates (dry-run, empty out, `--thread @none`, `ARTAGENTS_THREADS_OFF=1`, temp output, `upload.youtube`). Added `--thread`/`--variants`/`--from` to executor + orchestrator CLIs. `run.json` captures trimmed SD-008 fields. - **T4 — Attribution, lifecycle, prefix, CLI, reaper.** `threads/attribute.py` does the 5-branch decision under lock. `threads/prefix.py` emits `[thread]` and `[variants]` lines. `threads/cli.py` adds `thread new/list/show/archive/reopen/backfill/keep/group` (no `gc` per SD-036). Lazy reaper marks orphaned PIDs. Top-level `python3 -m artagents thread` route registered. - **T5 — Variants primitive.** Append-only `selections.jsonl` (last-write-wins), lock-protected `groups.json`. Only `role: variant | other` (no other roles). `thread keep`/`dismiss`/`group`. Variant prefix silenced after keep. **Only patched `executors/generate_image/run.py` and `orchestrators/logo_ideas/run.py`** for variant opt-in (SD-031 audit holds). - **T6 — Brief snapshots + provenance bridge.** `runs/<slug>/brief.copy.txt` + `brief_content_sha256`. Privacy stays path-based via `runs/<slug>/private/`. **The big one:** `threads/provenance.py` bridges existing `audit/ledger.jsonl` parent-IDs into typed `parent_run_ids` with artifact-hash fallback — exactly the architectural concern the gate raised. `hype.metadata.json` gets `pipeline.provenance` injected via the wrapper, not by editing forbidden executor `run.py` files. Net: Week 1 (data layer foundation) is **done**. T7-T15 are Week 2 — modality registry, iteration_prepare, iteration_assemble + render adapter, builtin.iteration_video orchestrator, dogfood/CI fixture, regression audit. The audit-bridge (T6) being live means the iteration-video phase has real provenance to walk, not the parallel-graph hand-wave I worried about earlier.

Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). If they pick **This session only**: - […]

<command-message>loop</command-message> <command-name>/loop</command-name> <command-args>Drive megaplan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 (code mode, robust, auto-approve) through prep→plan→critique→gate→[revise]→finalize→execute→review to completion. PLAN ID: implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 LAUNCHER: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ... EACH WAKE: 1. Check status: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('State:', d.get('state')); print('Iteration:', d.get('iteration')); print('Next step:', d.get('next_step')); print('Cost USD:', round(d.get('total_cost_usd',0),2)); last=d.get('last_step') or {}; print('Last step:', last.get('step'),'-',last.get('result'),'@',last.get('timestamp')); active=d.get('active_step') or {}; (print('Active:', active.get('step'),'agent='+str(active.get('agent')),'age='+str(active.get('age_seconds'))+'s','health='+str(active.get('health'))) if active else None); (print(' Progress:',active.get('phase_progress_summary')) if active else None); print('Notes:', d.get('notes_count'))" For execute also: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan progress --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); tasks=d.get('tasks',[]); from collections import Counter; c=Counter(t.get('status','?') for t in tasks); print('Batches:',dict(c))" 2. Decide by next_step (authoritative): - state in {"done","reviewed"}: LOOP COMPLETE. `git status --short`, summarize, report cost. STOP. - next_step == "review": run `megaplan review` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - next_step == "execute": run `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved` background. ScheduleWakeup 1800s. - next_step == "finalize": run `megaplan finalize` foreground. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "revise": if iteration > 4, STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan revise` background. ScheduleWakeup 1200s. - next_step == "gate": run `megaplan gate` foreground. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "critique": if last 2 critique results were "error", STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan critique` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "plan": run `megaplan plan` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "prep": run `megaplan prep` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - active_step healthy and active_step.step == "execute": allow up to 7200s (2hr — execute is heavy). ScheduleWakeup max(600, 7200-age). Override the 1800s generic stale rule for execute. - active_step healthy and other: allow up to 1800s. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800-age). - active_step age past max OR unhealthy: STOP and ask user. - Anything unexpected: stop loop, ask user. All commands need PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 prefix and --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020. 3. Relay state changes briefly. For execute, mention batch progress if changed. 4. NEVER ScheduleWakeup <= 300. 5. On stop, write "LOOP STOPPED: <reason>".</command-args>

ScheduleWakeup 1800s. - next_step == "finalize": run `megaplan finalize` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "revise": if iteration > 4, STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan revise` background. ScheduleWakeup 1200s. - next_step == "gate": run `megaplan gate` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "critique": if last 2 critique results were "error", STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan critique` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "plan": run `megaplan plan` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "prep": run `megaplan prep` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - active_step healthy and execute: allow up to 7200s (2hr). ScheduleWakeup max(600, 7200-age). - active_step healthy other: allow up to 1800s. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800-age). - active_step age past max OR unhealthy: STOP and ask user. - Anything unexpected: stop loop, ask user. All commands need PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 prefix and --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020. 3. Relay state changes briefly. For execute, mention batch progress if changed. 4. NEVER ScheduleWakeup <= 300. 5. On stop, write "LOOP STOPPED: <reason>".</command-args>

LAUNCHER: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan ... PLAN ID FOR ALL CALLS: implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 EACH WAKE, DO THIS: 1. Run: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan status --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('State:', d.get('state')); print('Iteration:', d.get('iteration')); print('Next step:', d.get('next_step')); print('Cost USD:', round(d.get('total_cost_usd',0),2)); last=d.get('last_step') or {}; print('Last step:', last.get('step'),'-',last.get('result'),'@',last.get('timestamp')); active=d.get('active_step') or {}; (print('Active:', active.get('step'),'agent='+str(active.get('agent')),'age='+str(active.get('age_seconds'))+'s','health='+str(active.get('health'))) if active else None); (print(' Progress:',active.get('phase_progress_summary')) if active else None); print('Notes:', d.get('notes_count'))" For execute also: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m megaplan progress --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020 2>&1 | python3 -c "import json,sys; d=json.load(sys.stdin); tasks=d.get('tasks',[]); from collections import Counter; c=Counter(t.get('status','?') for t in tasks); print('Batches:',dict(c))" 2. Decide by next_step: - state in {"done","reviewed"}: LOOP COMPLETE. `git status --short`, summarize, report cost. STOP. - next_step == "review": run `megaplan review` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "execute": run `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive --user-approved` background. ScheduleWakeup 1800s. - next_step == "finalize": run `megaplan finalize` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "revise": if iteration > 4, STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan revise` background. ScheduleWakeup 1200s. - next_step == "gate": run `megaplan gate` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "critique": if last 2 critique results were "error", STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan critique` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "plan": run `megaplan plan` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "prep": run `megaplan prep` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - active_step healthy and execute: allow up to 7200s (2hr). ScheduleWakeup max(600, 7200-age). - active_step healthy and other: allow up to 1800s. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800-age). - active_step age past max OR unhealthy: STOP and ask user. - Anything unexpected: stop loop, ask user. All commands need PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 prefix and --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020. 3. Relay state changes briefly. For execute, mention batch progress if changed. 4. NEVER ScheduleWakeup <= 300. 5. On stop, write "LOOP STOPPED: <reason>".</command-args>

ScheduleWakeup 900s. - active_step healthy and execute: allow up to 7200s (2hr). ScheduleWakeup max(600, 7200-age). - active_step healthy and other: allow up to 1800s. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800-age). - active_step age past max OR unhealthy: STOP and ask user. - Anything unexpected: stop loop, ask user. All commands need PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 prefix and --plan implement-the-10-day-sprint-20260503-2020. 3. Relay state changes briefly. For execute, mention batch progress if changed. 4. NEVER ScheduleWakeup <= 300. 5. On stop, write "LOOP STOPPED: <reason>".</command-args>

# /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it. ## Parsing (in priority order) 1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt. 2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). If they pick **This session only**: - […]

ScheduleWakeup 1800s. - next_step == "finalize": run `megaplan finalize` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "revise": if iteration > 4, STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan revise` background. ScheduleWakeup 1200s. - next_step == "gate": run `megaplan gate` background. ScheduleWakeup 600s. - next_step == "critique": if last 2 critique results were "error", STOP. Otherwise run `megaplan critique` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "plan": run `megaplan plan` background. ScheduleWakeup 1500s. - next_step == "prep": run `megaplan prep` background. ScheduleWakeup 900s. - active_step healthy and execute: allow up to 7200s (2hr). ScheduleWakeup max(600, 7200-age). - active_step healthy and other: allow up to 1800s. ScheduleWakeup max(600, 1800-age). - active_step age past max OR unhealthy: STOP and ask user. - Anything unexpected: stop loop, ask user. # /loop — schedule a recurring or self-paced prompt Parse the input below into `[interval] <prompt…>` and schedule it. ## Parsing (in priority order) 1. **Leading token**: if the first whitespace-delimited token matches `^\d+[smhd]$` (e.g. `5m`, `2h`), that's the interval; the rest is the prompt. 2. **Trailing "every" clause**: otherwise, if the input ends with `every <N><unit>` or `every <N> <unit-word>` (e.g. `every 20m`, `every 5 minutes`, `every 2 hours`), extract that as the interval and strip it from the prompt. Only match when what follows "every" is a time expression — `check every PR` has no interval. 3. **No interval**: otherwise, the entire input is the prompt and you'll self-pace dynamically (see "Dynamic mode" below). If the resulting prompt is empty, show usage `/loop [interval] <prompt>` and stop. Examples: - `5m /babysit-prs` → interval `5m`, prompt `/babysit-prs` (rule 1) - `check the deploy every 20m` → interval `20m`, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 2) - `run tests every 5 minutes` → interval `5m`, prompt `run tests` (rule 2) - `check the deploy` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check the deploy` (rule 3) - `check every PR` → no interval → dynamic mode, prompt `check every PR` (rule 3 — "every" not followed by time) - `5m` → empty prompt → show usage ## Offer cloud first Before any scheduling step, check whether EITHER is true: - the parsed interval (rule 1 or 2) is **≥60 minutes**, or - regardless of which rule matched, the original input uses daily phrasing ("every morning", "daily", "every day", "each night", "every weekday") If either is true, call AskUserQuestion first: - `question`: "This loop stops when you close this session. Set it up as a cloud schedule instead so it keeps running?" - `header`: "Schedule" - `options`: `[{label: "Cloud schedule (recommended)", description: "Runs in Anthropic's cloud even after you close this session"}, {label: "This session only", description: "Runs in this terminal until you exit"}]` If they pick **Cloud schedule**: do NOT call CronCreate. Invoke the `schedule` skill directly via the Skill tool with `args` set to their original input verbatim (e.g. `Skill({skill: "schedule", args: "every morning tell me a joke"})`), then follow that skill's instructions to completion. Do NOT tell the user to run /schedule themselves. **Then stop — do not continue to any section below** (no CronCreate, no ScheduleWakeup, no "execute the prompt now"). If they pick **This session only**: - […]

The 10-day sprint plan implementation completed. Here's what landed: ## New modules - **`artagents/threads/`** — the threads layer (12 files): `ids`, `schema`, `index` (fcntl.flock + atomic write + .bak), `record`, `wrapper`, `attribute`, `prefix` ([thread]/[variants] lines), `provenance` (audit→typed parent_run_ids bridge), `variants`, `cli`. - **`artagents/modalities/`** — registry: `image_grid`, `audio_waveform`, `generic_card` (loud fallback). - **`artagents/executors/iteration_prepare/`** — provenance-graph walk + data_quality. - **`artagents/executors/iteration_assemble/`** — emits `iteration.timeline.json` + render-compatible `hype.timeline.json` / `hype.assets.json`; quality-floor refusal at <0.6. - **`artagents/orchestrators/iteration_video/`** — chains prepare → assemble → render. ## Changes to existing code - Chokepoint wrappers in `artagents/executors/runner.py` and `artagents/orchestrators/runner.py` (writes run.json on every run). - `generate_image` and `logo_ideas` opt into `role: variant` per SD-031; everything else stays `role: other`. - Refactor: shared internals moved from `artagents/{executors,orchestrators,elements}/*.py` to `artagents/core/{executor,orchestrator,element}/`. ## Tests 23 new test files: `test_threads_*` (10), `test_iteration_*` (6), `test_modalities_registry`, `test_quality_floor`, `test_quality_zones`, plus dogfood fixture. ## Docs - `docs/threads.md` (new) - `SKILL.md` paragraph - `docs/sprint-thread-layer.md` finalized ## Run stats - **3 iterations** (plan converged: weighted_score 19.75 → 4.0 → 0) - **3 execute passes**: 15-batch initial run, scope_drift reconcile, then 1 SD-038 rework (T11 had touched `logo_ideas/STAGE.md`) - **Review:** 48/51 success criteria passed - **Cost reported by CLI:** $0.00 (subagent billing didn't surface through status) - **Working tree:** 60 modified/renamed + 4 new `__init__.py` files, all uncommitted Nothing's been committed yet — you have the full diff in your working tree to inspect or commit when ready.
